Abstract  Human demonstrations provide a rich source of bimanual dexterous skills, yet transferring them to robot hands remains challenging. Existing methods primarily optimize object trajectory tracking, often overlooking how human hands interact with the object. Even with accurate tracking, robots may contact incorrect object parts or slide their fingers, resulting in unstable manipulation. We present DexDuet, a framework that learns contact-preserving bimanual dexterity from a single human demonstration. DexDuet employs two interaction rewards to preserve human hand-object configurations and contacts. To enable coordinated manipulation, we factorize the bimanual policy into two weight-sharing per-hand policies that condition on each other's states. We further propose assisted reference-state initialization (A-RSI), an adaptive curriculum that adjusts object assistance and training starts across demonstration segments to overcome difficult manipulation phases. Across four benchmarks spanning three simulators and five robot embodiments, DexDuet improves object-tracking ADD-AUC by 11 to 41 points over the corresponding baselines while consistently achieving better contact preservation. Notably, among 122 paired runs with comparable object-tracking accuracy, DexDuet preserves the human's contacts better in 117 cases. Code and videos are available at https://rcl-robotics.github.io/Dexduet.io/
[24.11.2026 13:36] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет DexDuet — фреймворк, который обучает двуручную ловкость роботических рук по одной демонстрации человека с сохранением контактов. Основная идея в том, что простого отслеживания траектории объекта недостаточно, поэтому вводятся два интерактивных reward, сохраняющих конфигурацию руки относительно объекта и контакты. Двуручная политика факторизуется на две политики для каждой руки с разделяемыми весами, которые учитывают состояние друг друга. Адаптивный curriculum (A-RSI) регулирует помощь с объектом и стартовые состояния по сегментам демонстрации, а на четырёх бенчмарках DexDuet улучшает ADD-AUC на 11–41 пункт и лучше сохраняет контакты.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Роботы учатся двуручной ловкости, сохраняя контакт руки с объектом"}

Existing methods primarily optimize object trajectory tracking, often overlooking how human hands interact with the object. We present DexDuet, a framework that learns contact-preserving bimanual dexterity from a single human demonstration. DexDuet employs two interaction rewards to preserve human hand-object configurations and contacts. To enable coordinated manipulation, we factorize the bimanual policy into two weight-sharing per-hand policies that condition on each other's states. We further propose assisted reference-state initialization (A-RSI), an adaptive curriculum that adjusts object assistance and training starts across demonstration segments to overcome difficult manipulation phases. Across four benchmarks spanning three simulators and five robot embodiments, DexDuet improves object-tracking ADD-AUC by 11 to 41 points over the corresponding baselines while consistently achieving better contact preservation. Notably, among 122 paired runs with comparable object-tracking accuracy, DexDuet preserves the human's contacts better in 117 cases. Code and videos are available at https://rcl-robotics.github.io/Dexduet.io/"

Abstract  Scaling device-control evaluation requires scaling both interactive worlds and the tasks agents must solve. We introduce SynthDCB, a benchmark for environment awareness and device control built through world distillation (WD). World distillation transforms knowledge of device behaviors, constraints, and environmental effects into modular executable components. A unified capability representation connects tool discovery, world execution, and task synthesis. SynthDCB instantiates Home, Car, and Phone with 114 device classes, 1,166 functions, and 95 events, supporting five task families with 32 subtypes. Explicit states and tool-call traces support task validation and agent evaluation. We evaluate 22 models on 2,560 test tasks; the best achieves 88.8% mean Pass@1. Construction experiments demonstrate token efficiency and expansion of task coverage. At the richest knowledge level, WD fully covers 22/23 properties, 19/29 functionalities, and 30/39 event requirements specified in a real RobotVacuum protocol. Furthermore, the 1,000 sampled tasks semantically cover 81.6% of the 854 reference instructions in the real-world corpus. SynthDCB connects an expanding space of real-to-sim executable capabilities to verifiable agent evaluation.

[24.11.2026 13:37] OpenRouter request. Model: anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5. Abstract A hierarchical memory system using a vision-language model to select keyframes and an executor to act on them improves long-horizon robotic manipulation by retaining sparse visual details.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Robotic manipulation often requires remembering visual information that is no longer observable, such as the original location of an object. Existing approaches typically rely on full observation histories, which become redundant over long horizons, or text summaries, which may omit precise visual details. We study visual moment memory, the ability to retain such details from key moments for subsequent manipulation. To support this study, we introduce VMBench, a suite of long-horizon manipulation tasks requiring memory of spatial locations, visual appearances, and their associations. We further propose MomentMem, a hierarchical architecture in which a VLM-based memorizer selects keyframes and an executor predicts actions conditioned on the selected memories. This separation enables us to evaluate memory selection alongside closed-loop task success and to study memorizer generalization independently of the executor. On VMBench, MomentMem achieves 49.9% average success, outperforming the strongest evaluated baseline by 31.7 percentage points. Moreover, we find that the memorizer transfers well to unseen appearances zero-shot. It can also adapt to an unseen task with only a few memory annotations, reaching 43.7% success, close to the 44.3% obtained with full memory supervision. Further experiments show competitive performance on RMBench and improvements in real-robot manipulation. These results highlight the value of retaining sparse visual evidence for memory-dependent manipulation. Our code is available at https://github.com/PKU-VaLuE-Lab/MomentMem.

Авторы вводят понятие visual moment memory и бенчмарк VMBench, а также иерархическую архитектуру MomentMem, где VLM-based memorizer выбирает ключевые кадры, а executor предсказывает действия на их основе. Такое разделение позволяет оценивать качество отбора памяти отдельно от успеха выполнения и исследовать генерализацию memorizer на неизвестные внешние виды и задачи. Abstract Loop-PP schedules cyclic pipeline stages for depth-recurrent models, overlapping communication and compute while preserving exact numerical equivalence, reducing memory and increasing throughput on MoE models.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Depth-recurrent (Looped) models raise effective depth by applying a shared stack of blocks several times. Scaling them usually requires pipeline parallelism, but commonly used interleaved 1F1B is drawn for an acyclic stage graph in which each micro-batch enters each stage once. Once that stack is split across stages, the same stage is visited several times on one micro-batch and later stages feed earlier ones: native 1F1B has no slot for the extra visit, cannot rank a first iteration against a second, and its neighbour-only send/receive order has no back edge to the first stage, so the arrangement stalls or fires out of order. We present Loop-PP, which rebuilds both the timetable and the communication for this cyclic layout. It schedules different iterations of the same stage separately, specializes a 1F1B skeleton into a globally consistent order over those visits, and emits a static instruction stream per rank that separates posting from waiting so communication overlaps local compute. Loop-PP is bit-identical to a single-stage reference. On 8B-60B MoE models, it lowers peak memory by as much as 32.8\%, and with compute budget fixed, the best observed throughput rises by 12.7\%.
[24.11.2026 13:37] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет Loop-PP — новый способ распределения рекуррентных моделей глубины (Looped models) по стадиям pipeline parallelism, где один и тот же блок применяется несколько раз. Классический interleaved 1F1B рассчитан на ациклический граф стадий, поэтому при циклическом проходе micro-batch он не может корректно упорядочить итерации и приводит к зависаниям или неверному порядку. Loop-PP перестраивает расписание, разделяя итерации одной стадии и выстраивая глобально согласованный порядок, а также генерирует статический поток инструкций, где отправка и ожидание данных разделены, что позволяет перекрывать коммуникацию с локальными вычислениями. Метод численно идентичен однostadийной эталонной реализации, а на MoE-моделях от 8B до 60B параметров снижает пиковое потребление памяти до 32.8% и повышает пропускную способность до 12.7% при фиксированном бюджете вычислений.", "emoji": "🔁", "title": "Циклы без простоя: конвейер, который умеет возвращаться назад"}

Abstract Hindsight Twins improves text-to-image alignment by distilling the velocity difference between twin prompt revisions while avoiding critic-induced bias.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Text-to-image models can learn from their own mistakes by distilling a self-teacher conditioned on a vision-language critic's revised prompt. However, such self-teachers barely improve alignment, and both they and their hindsight-contrast variants absorb the critic's unintended additions to the scene, such as lighting or scenery. We attribute this to two factors: (1) the prompt already specifies the desired fix, so a useful revision must go beyond the failed span, often rewriting the entire scene and adding details that overshadow the fix; (2) although the critic localizes the error to a single span, self-teachers condition on the rewrite, entangling the intended fix with unintended additions. Motivated by these insights, we propose Hindsight Twins (HinT), which fills the critic's revision template once with the intended fix and once with the observed error and distills only their velocity difference, anchored at the user's prompt. Because text encoders spread the edit of a single slot to all other tokens, HinT further aligns the twins' token states outside the slot. Across Qwen-Image-2512, SD3.5-M, and FLUX.2-klein, HinT keeps the rate of critic-induced bias at the base-model level while matching or exceeding the alignment gains of prior self-distillation, and it keeps improving over twelve rounds of self-training where contrastive baselines decay.
[24.11.2026 13:37] Response: {"desc": "Статья предлагает метод Hindsight Twins (HinT) для улучшения соответствия текста и изображения в text-to-image моделях. Paper text to classify:

"Abstract Hindsight Twins improves text-to-image alignment by distilling the velocity difference between twin prompt revisions while avoiding critic-induced bias.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Text-to-image models can learn from their own mistakes by distilling a self-teacher conditioned on a vision-language critic's revised prompt. However, such self-teachers barely improve alignment, and both they and their hindsight-contrast variants absorb the critic's unintended additions to the scene, such as lighting or scenery. We attribute this to two factors: (1) the prompt already specifies the desired fix, so a useful revision must go beyond the failed span, often rewriting the entire scene and adding details that overshadow the fix; (2) although the critic localizes the error to a single span, self-teachers condition on the rewrite, entangling the intended fix with unintended additions. Motivated by these insights, we propose Hindsight Twins (HinT), which fills the critic's revision template once with the intended fix and once with the observed error and distills only their velocity difference, anchored at the user's prompt. Because text encoders spread the edit of a single slot to all other tokens, HinT further aligns the twins' token states outside the slot. Across Qwen-Image-2512, SD3.5-M, and FLUX.2-klein, HinT keeps the rate of critic-induced bias at the base-model level while matching or exceeding the alignment gains of prior self-distillation, and it keeps improving over twelve rounds of self-training where contrastive baselines decay."

Abstract  Recent multimodal search agents use reasoning, vision, and live web search across many turns to answer questions beyond parametric knowledge by orchestrating a small library of hand-crafted search and visual tools. However, the library is laborious to create and maintain, and frozen when new query types, evidence formats, and failure modes are encountered in the wild. Human practitioners, by contrast, cope with insufficient tools by fashioning new ones from familiar parts and reusing them across tasks. Inspired by this, we propose ToolSmith, an automatic tool-generation framework that grows and reuses modules on top of a lean base tool library. We evaluate ToolSmith on three in-domain multimodal search benchmarks and ten out-of-domain benchmarks under a VLM-as-a-Judge protocol. ToolSmith consistently improves over the base library across deployed agents of varying scales, with larger backbones reaping greater gains, demonstrating the scalability of the approach. Our code and data are available at https://threesr.github.io/toolsmith/.

Abstract  Most LLM agent frameworks build a crew that runs a task and exits. Physical environments (e.g., homes, labs, factory floors) need agents that stay up for months, survive their own bugs, and run on whatever hardware is already next to the sensors. We present Wactorz, an open-source runtime in which LLM-generated agents are long-lived, supervised actors: a planner turns a chat request into Python, compiles it into a new actor at runtime, and a supervisor restarts that actor alone, with its state intact, when it crashes. All coordination rides on MQTT, so agents can be spawned on, and live-migrated between, edge nodes such as a Raspberry Pi, and every internal event is a topic external systems can subscribe to. The recorded demo builds a camera-to-lamp automation from one chat request, adds a second agent that is auto-wired to the first by topic and carries a deliberate division-by-zero, watches that agent repair its own code without losing its count, then deploys a fresh Raspberry Pi node and live-migrates the agent onto it, in under four minutes of wall-clock time and \$0.13 of model usage. This extended version adds what the two-page demonstration paper could not: the Wiring view of the topic graph, Wactorz as a library for hand-written agents, how trained models are stored, sent to nodes and replaced, the security model, and the system's limitations.
[24.11.2026 13:37] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет Wactorz — открытый runtime, в котором агенты на базе LLM реализованы как долгоживущие супервизируемые акторы, которые создаются во время работы из Python-кода, сгенерированного планировщиком по запросу в чате. Супервизор перезапускает упавший актор с сохранением его состояния, а вся координация идёт через MQTT, что позволяет запускать агенты и переносить их между edge-узлами, например Raspberry Pi, без остановки. Paper text to classify:

"Abstract  Most LLM agent frameworks build a crew that runs a task and exits. Physical environments (e.g., homes, labs, factory floors) need agents that stay up for months, survive their own bugs, and run on whatever hardware is already next to the sensors. We present Wactorz, an open-source runtime in which LLM-generated agents are long-lived, supervised actors: a planner turns a chat request into Python, compiles it into a new actor at runtime, and a supervisor restarts that actor alone, with its state intact, when it crashes. All coordination rides on MQTT, so agents can be spawned on, and live-migrated between, edge nodes such as a Raspberry Pi, and every internal event is a topic external systems can subscribe to. The recorded demo builds a camera-to-lamp automation from one chat request, adds a second agent that is auto-wired to the first by topic and carries a deliberate division-by-zero, watches that agent repair its own code without losing its count, then deploys a fresh Raspberry Pi node and live-migrates the agent onto it, in under four minutes of wall-clock time and \$0.13 of model usage. This extended version adds what the two-page demonstration paper could not: the Wiring view of the topic graph, Wactorz as a library for hand-written agents, how trained models are stored, sent to nodes and replaced, the security model, and the system's limitations."

Abstract PersistWorld maintains a scene-anchored 3D world state updated from camera streams and forecasts future semantic occupancy more accurately and faster than prior camera-based world models.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash 4D occupancy forecasting predicts the 3D semantic occupancy of a driving scene seconds ahead. Over a few seconds, the scene changes little while the vehicle moves, so most of the space ahead has already been seen. Sorting the future into unchanged, changed, and unseen space, we find that current camera-based world models fall short on the unchanged part: they fail to hold on to what they have already seen, rather than to forecast what will change. We therefore make what the model has seen its state. PersistWorld streams camera frames into a persistent 3D world state anchored in the scene, writing each frame once instead of rebuilding the state from past frames. The state is learned end to end with the forecast, and an evolver predicts it at any queried time along the ego trajectory. On Occ3D-nuScenes, PersistWorld raises the IoU of the best camera-based world model by 19 points at 1-3 s and by 18 to 24 points up to 8 s, while running more than twice as fast. Code and models: https://github.com/wangzian828/PersistWorld. Project page: https://wangzian828.github.io/PersistWorld
[24.11.2026 13:38] Response: {"desc": "PersistWorld — это camera-based world model для прогнозирования 4D occupancy в автономном вождении, которая хранит в памяти постоянное 3D-состояние сцены, привязанное к пространству. Вместо того чтобы пересобирать состояние из прошлых кадров, модель записывает каждый кадр в это состояние однократно и обучает его end-to-end вместе с прогнозом. Эволвер предсказывает состояние в любой момент времени вдоль траектории ego-автомобиля, что помогает удерживать уже увиденную, неизменившуюся часть пространства. На Occ3D-nuScenes модель повышает IoU лучшей камерной world model на 18-24 пункта и работает более чем вдвое быстрее.", "emoji": "🧭", "title": "Запоминать увиденное, а не пересчитывать его заново"}

Abstract ReWeave jointly optimizes an embodied agent harness and an emergent physical task topology from interaction without updating policy weights, improving success and enabling continual learning across tasks.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Long-horizon embodied tasks involve physical dependencies that may only become clear through interaction. As task understanding deepens, past trials can reveal new information for adaptation and transfer. However, coarse progress feedback cannot identify which physical preconditions held, leaving search unguided, while task-specific optimization easily overfits to individual scenes, making cross-task transfer brittle. We introduce ReWeave, a framework that jointly optimizes an embodied agent harness and an emergent physical task topology from interaction without updating policy weights. Keeping execution policies and perception frozen, ReWeave co-optimizes coupled context, workflows, and control loops. As new evidence revises the topology, ReWeave reprojects past trials onto the updated task understanding, exposing retained progress, regressions, and bottlenecks that guide parent selection and search width. Across tasks, matching verified local mechanisms to unmet preconditions consolidates 72 specialist units into 44 reusable groups (0.34times full concatenation) within a compact shared harness capable of continual learning. ReWeave improves held-out success from 89.00% to 98.75% on LIBERO-PRO and from 25.50% to 58.25% on RoboDojo, while uncovering retained progress in 89% of 159 failed RoboDojo development trials and translating stronger offline reasoning into higher online performance. On eight unseen LIBERO-PRO tasks, the shared harness reaches 51.3% success versus 41.3% for direct distillation, rising to 75.0% after continual learning.

Замороженные политики исполнения и perception дополняются координированной оптимизацией контекста, workflow и control loops, а новые данные пересматривают топологию и переносят прошлые trial'ы на обновлённое понимание задачи. Abstract  Vision-language-action (VLA) models leverage pretrained vision-language representations for instruction-conditioned robot control, yet their understanding of task-relevant 3D relations and how these relations evolve during manipulation remains largely implicit. To address this challenge, we introduce WeaveVLA, a framework that enhances VLA action generation by explicitly modeling task-relevant relative 3D relations and maintaining relational context across observations. WeaveVLA employs a task-conditioned sparse graph neural network to encode relative 3D relations among objects, the gripper, and nearby references. Pair-specific recurrent edge states integrate geometric evidence across observations to maintain evolving spatial context. A learned-query readout summarizes the updated graph representations into compact relational tokens, which condition the action expert alongside the original vision-language context. An auxiliary geometry reconstruction objective further encourages spatial structure preservation. We instantiate WeaveVLA on StarVLA-GR00T and π_{0.5}, achieving over 98\% average success on LIBERO with both backbones. On LIBERO-Plus, WeaveVLA improves success from 74.9\% to 82.0\% and from 85.7\% to 87.8\%, respectively, with additional improvements on CALVIN DrightarrowD. On three real-world tasks, the StarVLA-GR00T-based variant improves average success from 55.6\% to 70.0\% under standard conditions and from 33.3\% to 53.3\% under OOD perturbations, surpassing the π_{0.5} baseline in both settings.
[24.11.2026 13:38] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлен WeaveVLA — фреймворк, который дополняет модели vision-language-action (VLA) явным моделированием относительных 3D-отношений между объектами, захватом робота и опорными точками. Для этого используется task-conditioned sparse graph neural network с рекуррентными состояниями рёбер, которые накапливают геометрическую информацию по последовательности наблюдений. Полученные графовые представления сворачиваются в компактные relational tokens и подаются в action expert вместе с исходным контекстом vision-language. Вспомогательная задача реконструкции геометрии помогает сохранять пространственную структуру, а результаты на LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, CALVIN и трёх реальных задачах показывают заметный рост успешности, в том числе в условиях OOD-возмущений.", "emoji": "🕸️", "title": "Явные 3D-связи помогают роботу не терять пространственный контекст при манипуляциях"}

Abstract MORSE leverages recurring failures to search for adaptable methods and pretrained perception models, improving accuracy and speed of a frozen video-language model on streaming video question answering.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash A streaming video system must decide in a single pass what evidence to retain under bounded frame storage, before it knows which details a future question will require. Recent recursive self-improvement methods automate the design of the harness that stores and retrieves this evidence. They revise the harness in response to failures, but the editor must still devise each repair from its prior knowledge and a predefined set of tools. A failure that recurs across videos, however, may point to a problem that prior work has already addressed. We introduce MORSE (Memory Optimization with Recursive Self-Evolvement), an open-world auto-research framework that uses recurring failures to guide an online search for solutions. Its editor groups failures in training traces by cause, then searches papers, model cards, and code for methods it can adapt or pretrained perception models it can use. When the editor introduces a new perceptual capability, it can also revise how the harness stores and retrieves the resulting evidence for a frozen video-language model. On held-out validation streams, MORSE first selects revisions that improve answer quality, then seeks faster revisions that preserve those gains within a specified tolerance. Across three streaming video benchmarks, MORSE consistently achieves the highest answer accuracy within each frozen-backbone comparison. On OVO-Bench, our Qwen3-VL-8B harness answers queries in 0.30 seconds on average while achieving 75.1% accuracy, 3.1 percentage points ahead of the next-best evaluated harness using the same frozen backbone.

Редактор группирует ошибки по причинам, ищет методы в статьях, model cards и коде, а также подключает готовые perception-модели. Затем он оптимизирует harness хранения и поиска кадров при замороженном backbone, сначала повышая точность ответов, а затем ускоряя систему в заданных пределах допуска. Abstract Feedback Shortcut reduces speculative decoding errors caused by feeding top-layer hidden states back into Transformer inputs by aligning projected states and correcting drafts with predecessor states, preserving exact-decoding quality while accelerating decoding.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Feeding each position's top-layer hidden state back into the next position's input improves Transformer performance. However, this feedback breaks speculative decoding, since verifying a drafted token requires the top-layer state from its preceding position, which forces the draft to rely on an approximate stand-in. Training the model to adapt to this stand-in can lower the exact-decoding performance ceiling, while using it at inference time further introduces approximation error. We propose Feedback Shortcut to address both bottlenecks. First, state alignment trains a lightweight projection from multi-token prediction head states to stand-ins, leaving base model training untouched. Second, pipelined correction re-evaluates each draft using its predecessor's newly computed state, a much closer proxy to true feedback. Running this correction inside the verification pass of subsequent drafts improves accuracy with zero extra calls. Across 135M and 780M scales, Feedback Shortcut stays within 10^{-4} bits per byte of the exact-decoding ceiling on Paloma and accelerates decoding by 2.4-2.8x with about 2% more training compute than the latent feedback Transformer. At large batch sizes, skipping correction still preserves Transformer speed and over 80% of latent feedback's gains.
[24.11.2026 13:38] Response: {"desc": "Статья посвящена ускорению декодирования Transformer-моделей с обратной связью по скрытым состояниям верхнего слоя, которая ломает speculative decoding. Авторы предлагают Feedback Shortcut: state alignment обучает лёгкую проекцию из состояний multi-token prediction head в приближённые заменители обратной связи, не трогая обучение базовой модели. Второй компонент, pipelined correction, пересчитывает каждый черновик с использованием нового состояния предшествующего токена, что повышает точность без дополнительных вызовов модели. Метод сохраняет качество exact decoding и ускоряет генерацию в 2.4–2.8 раза при небольшом росте вычислений на обучение.", "emoji": "⚡", "title": "Быстрая генерация без потери качества: обратная связь, которая не тормозит декодирование"}

Abstract  Typhoon OCR 1.5 focused on reading Thai documents. However, in real-world workflows, users often need to extract specific information or preserve the structure of an entire page for further processing. Typhoon OCR 2 handles both tasks in a single model, with prompt-driven extraction and structured full-page parsing. Full-page parsing covers Thai, English, and Chinese. We release compact 2B and 0.8B models adapted from open document parsers through supervised fine-tuning on curated Thai and public multilingual data, followed by Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with task rewards and a rule penalizing unfinished responses. Among evaluated open models, Typhoon OCR 2 2B scores highest on Thai MDPBench (80.7 versus 68.9 for Typhoon OCR 1.5) and on ThaiOCRBench (59.91 versus 40.45), which covers recognition, extraction, and document understanding. The 2B model remains close to its base on English olmOCR-bench (84.7 versus 84.4) and on OmniDocBench, which evaluates English and Chinese together (90.97 versus 91.03). On MDPBench, GRPO reduces the mean share of pages with repetition loops for the 2B model from 3.2% to 0.7%, with a smaller and statistically uncertain score gain for the 0.8B model. We release the model weights, Python package, and evaluation suite.
[24.11.2026 13:38] Response: {"desc": "Typhoon OCR 2 — это компактная мультимодальная LLM для распознавания документов, которая объединяет извлечение отдельной информации по промпту и структурированный разбор всей страницы в одной модели. Модель обучена на тайском, английском и китайском языках через supervised fine-tuning с последующей оптимизацией GRPO, где используются награды за качество задачи и штраф за незавершённые ответы. Версия 2B превосходит предыдущую версию 1.5 и другие открытые модели на тайских бенчмарках MDPBench и ThaiOCRBench, а на английском и китайском сохраняет уровень базовой модели. GRPO также заметно снижает долю страниц с зацикливанием повторов, с 3.2% до 0.7%, а модели и инструменты оценки опубликованы в открытом доступе.", "emoji": "📄", "title": "Один OCR-ассистент для тайских документов: извлечение и разбор страниц в одной модели"}

Abstract A sample-efficient curation method and a structure-aware layout metric enable a fine-tuned vision-language model to improve OCR on challenging financial document patterns, achieving high word recall and document question-answering F1.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Optical Character Recognition (OCR) pipelines and vision-language models exhibit high accuracy on standard OCR and document-understanding benchmarks, yet they systematically underperform on document patterns that are operationally critical in financial workflows. These patterns include vertical or rotated text, logo-embedded vendor names, fine-print regulatory clauses, degraded scans, and complex multi-column layouts. While underrepresented in public datasets, they constitute a substantial portion of real-world failure cases. We introduce SAVIOR, a sample-efficient data curation methodology that targets such high-impact failure scenarios to adapt vision-language models for robust financial OCR. We further propose PaIRS, a structure-aware evaluation metric that measures layout fidelity by comparing pairwise spatial relationships between tokens. Fine-tuning Qwen2.5-VL-Instruct with SAVIOR-Train achieves a word recall of 0.93. When the resulting layout-aware OCR output is used as context for document question answering, it attains an F1 score of 0.89, outperforming both open- and closed-source models such as GPT-4o, Mistral-OCR, PaddleOCR-VL, and DeepSeek-OCR.
[24.11.2026 13:38] Response: {"desc": "Авторы показывают, что современные OCR-системы и vision-language модели плохо распознают важные для финансов элементы документов, такие как повернутый текст, логотипы, мелкий шрифт и сложные многоколоночные макеты. Для адаптации модели Qwen2.5-VL-Instruct они предлагают метод SAVIOR, который отбирает небольшой и целенаправленный обучающий набор из проблемных примеров. "Abstract A sample-efficient curation method and a structure-aware layout metric enable a fine-tuned vision-language model to improve OCR on challenging financial document patterns, achieving high word recall and document question-answering F1.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Optical Character Recognition (OCR) pipelines and vision-language models exhibit high accuracy on standard OCR and document-understanding benchmarks, yet they systematically underperform on document patterns that are operationally critical in financial workflows. These patterns include vertical or rotated text, logo-embedded vendor names, fine-print regulatory clauses, degraded scans, and complex multi-column layouts. While underrepresented in public datasets, they constitute a substantial portion of real-world failure cases. We introduce SAVIOR, a sample-efficient data curation methodology that targets such high-impact failure scenarios to adapt vision-language models for robust financial OCR. We further propose PaIRS, a structure-aware evaluation metric that measures layout fidelity by comparing pairwise spatial relationships between tokens. Fine-tuning Qwen2.5-VL-Instruct with SAVIOR-Train achieves a word recall of 0.93. When the resulting layout-aware OCR output is used as context for document question answering, it attains an F1 score of 0.89, outperforming both open- and closed-source models such as GPT-4o, Mistral-OCR, PaddleOCR-VL, and DeepSeek-OCR."

Abstract A unified power-law scoring rule for cache eviction calibrates one exponent per model and yields a method that improves language-model perplexity and preserves question-answering accuracy without dataset-specific tuning.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash The decay exponent α in KV-cache eviction is an intrinsic property of the model: calibrated once on a single corpus, it transfers directly across domains. Attention-based accumulation (H2O) and recency-based sliding windows (StreamingLLM) are two dominant eviction frameworks, often treated as unrelated, but we show that they are the two endpoints of a single power-law family: by scoring each token as s_i = A_i cdot (t-i)^{-α}, where A_i is cumulative attention and α controls the age penalty, H2O and StreamingLLM correspond to α=0 and αtoinfty, respectively (with StreamingLLM additionally hard-coding an attention-sink patch).   The no-hair mechanism of xiang2026nohair bounds the admissible range of α, and the measured exponents agree: calibrating once on WikiText yields [0.556, 0.822] for a representative model (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct). The per-model ranges are consistent across five models (410M--1.5B) and independently match the optimal regions from perplexity sweeps.   NoHairCache achieves the lowest perplexity across three datasets (WikiText, PG19, and arXiv) without per-dataset tuning, outperforming both H2O and StreamingLLM (p < 0.05 on all three datasets, with 0.06--0.10 PPL gains over StreamingLLM at budget 512). On downstream four-way multiple-choice QA, it matches or outperforms StreamingLLM across all nine (task, budget) cells, retaining 98.2% of full-cache accuracy on NarrativeQA at 25% retention versus 91.1% for StreamingLLM; per-cell McNemar tests are not significant, so QA is corroborating only. We release our calibration script and drop-in scorer upon publication.

Значение α калибруется один раз для каждой модели на одном корпусе (WikiText) и затем переносится на другие домены без дополнительной настройки. Метод NoHairCache демонстрирует наименьшую perplexity на WikiText, PG19 и arXiv и сохраняет точность в задачах question answering, например 98.2% от полного кэша на NarrativeQA при 25% удержании. Abstract  Hierarchical text detection aims to localize words, text lines, and paragraphs while parsing their structural relationships. It provides essential structural cues for visual text understanding. To maximize accuracy, existing methods typically rely on complex multi-scale feature fusion necks and heavy-weight heads, resulting in bloated architectures and low inference efficiency. In this paper, we argue that a sufficiently pre-trained backbone can effectively replace these sophisticated external modules, while simple geometric inclusion suffices for hierarchical text structural parsing. Thus, we propose Prism, a minimalist framework for hierarchical text detection. Prism performs multi-granularity detection by attaching parallel lightweight heads to a single-scale backbone feature map, covering words, text lines, and paragraphs, and reconstructs the structural hierarchy via an efficient geometric inclusion algorithm. In addition, we systematically compare representative lightweight head archetypes and show that pixel-level regression offers the best accuracy-efficiency trade-off in this setting. Experiments on multiple public benchmarks show that our significantly simplified architecture achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance with remarkable efficiency. Specifically, it runs 1.7times faster than LRANet on CTW1500 while achieving higher accuracy, and surpasses the current SOTA method, OmniParser V2, by over 6 PQ points on the hierarchical HierText dataset. Code will be released soon.

Вместо сложных многомасштабных necks и тяжёлых голов модель использует предобученный backbone с одной картой признаков и параллельные легковесные головы. Paper text to classify:

"Abstract  Hierarchical text detection aims to localize words, text lines, and paragraphs while parsing their structural relationships. It provides essential structural cues for visual text understanding. To maximize accuracy, existing methods typically rely on complex multi-scale feature fusion necks and heavy-weight heads, resulting in bloated architectures and low inference efficiency. In this paper, we argue that a sufficiently pre-trained backbone can effectively replace these sophisticated external modules, while simple geometric inclusion suffices for hierarchical text structural parsing. Thus, we propose Prism, a minimalist framework for hierarchical text detection. Prism performs multi-granularity detection by attaching parallel lightweight heads to a single-scale backbone feature map, covering words, text lines, and paragraphs, and reconstructs the structural hierarchy via an efficient geometric inclusion algorithm. In addition, we systematically compare representative lightweight head archetypes and show that pixel-level regression offers the best accuracy-efficiency trade-off in this setting. Experiments on multiple public benchmarks show that our significantly simplified architecture achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance with remarkable efficiency. Specifically, it runs 1.7times faster than LRANet on CTW1500 while achieving higher accuracy, and surpasses the current SOTA method, OmniParser V2, by over 6 PQ points on the hierarchical HierText dataset. Code will be released soon."

Abstract  Graphical user interface (GUI) agents are widely trained in executable GUI environments. To lower cost, build environments in batches, and reduce manual effort, each work trades away part of the environment through its construction choices. We describe these choices along three dimensions: reward coverage, the goal-reaching trajectories the verifier rewards; data coverage, the application data the agent is exposed to beyond what the task reads or writes; and feature coverage, the application features beyond what the task uses. Existing works set each dimension on engineering grounds, but they do not measure its effect on training. How complete must an environment be to train a capable agent, and along which dimensions? In MobileGym, we adjust each dimension separately, train Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct, and evaluate every policy in the original full environment. A verifier that rewards only one reference path lowers success by 10.53 points, and removing unneeded records also lowers success. Pruning unneeded features instead raises success from 51.61% to 56.13%, entirely on tasks whose environments it left unchanged. The gain reverses when each episode is weighted equally and vanishes when only episodes on the pruned tasks are balanced. Without pruning, length weighting there reinforces long detour successes that erode skills on other tasks. Following these findings, we build MobileGym-Lite, which keeps reward and data coverage complete and prunes unneeded features. It outperforms Full in the full environment (55.48% versus 51.61% success) with lower construction cost.

"Abstract  Graphical user interface (GUI) agents are widely trained in executable GUI environments. To lower cost, build environments in batches, and reduce manual effort, each work trades away part of the environment through its construction choices. We describe these choices along three dimensions: reward coverage, the goal-reaching trajectories the verifier rewards; data coverage, the application data the agent is exposed to beyond what the task reads or writes; and feature coverage, the application features beyond what the task uses. Existing works set each dimension on engineering grounds, but they do not measure its effect on training. How complete must an environment be to train a capable agent, and along which dimensions? In MobileGym, we adjust each dimension separately, train Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct, and evaluate every policy in the original full environment. Pruning unneeded features instead raises success from 51.61% to 56.13%, entirely on tasks whose environments it left unchanged. Without pruning, length weighting there reinforces long detour successes that erode skills on other tasks. Following these findings, we build MobileGym-Lite, which keeps reward and data coverage complete and prunes unneeded features. It outperforms Full in the full environment (55.48% versus 51.61% success) with lower construction cost."

Abstract Fast-JEPA is a latent world model planning framework that improves the success-latency trade-off of predictive search by predicting complete action-sequence outcomes and using goal-directed plans, with evaluation on manipulation and navigation tasks and a new dynamic planning benchmark.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Latent world models predict the outcomes of action sequences, but comparing candidate plans through recursive rollout remains expensive at test time. We introduce Fast-JEPA, a latent world model planning framework that advances the success-latency Pareto frontier of predictive search. Fast-JEPA reorganizes predictive computation to support effective action-sequence search at low planning cost. It constructs a goal-directed plan to guide search and directly predicts the outcomes of complete candidates for efficient comparison, avoiding recursive state rollout during refinement. Across PushT, Reacher, Cube, and TwoRoom, Fast-JEPA achieves 96.50% average success rate with 2.41x faster planning than INTACT. Search-budget sweeps show that its competitive success and low latency extend across candidate counts and refinement depths. We further introduce DynaGoal, a benchmark for evaluating world-model planning under dynamic out-of-distribution conditions, execution deadlines, and controlled response delays. Fast-JEPA achieves average success rates of 73.83% on DynaGoal-Shift and 87.67% on DynaGoal-Deadline, demonstrating the practical value of low-latency predictive planning under changing control conditions and execution-time constraints. Our code is available at https://github.com/Sofilyzia/Fast-JEPA.

Abstract Paired non-contrast and contrast-enhanced medical images reveal that visual content retrieved through value vectors, rather than spatial attention alone, is critical for multimodal model perception, motivating a distillation method that improves non-contrast inference.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Current MLLM methods largely focus on whether models attend to the right visual regions, implicitly treating spatial attention map as the primary key of effective visual perception. To examine this assumption, we introduce a medical VQA dataset with paired non-contrast (NC) and contrast-enhanced (CE) images that share corresponding anatomy while providing different cues. We show that this view is incomplete: across paired NC and CE medical images, MLLMs often exhibit similar attention patterns despite producing contrasting answers, while controlled bidirectional interventions induce substantially larger prediction changes on visual values than attention weights. These results identify the visual content retrieved through values as a critical factor in improving perception beyond spatial attention alone. Guided by this finding, we propose VIVID (VIsual Value Intervention Distillation), which uses enhanced visual values to identify and refine teacher corrections aligned with useful visual evidence to improve on-policy distillation from CE to NC images. Across six internal and external test sets, VIVID consistently outperforms existing on-policy distillation methods across different model scales and architectures, requires only NC images at inference, and maintains strong zero-shot performance on general-domain VQA benchmarks. The project page is available at https://qiuzyc.github.io/VIVID.

Для этого создан медицинский VQA-датасет с парами снимков без контраста (NC) и с контрастированием (CE), а двунаправленные интервенции показывают, что изменение values сильнее влияет на предсказания, чем изменение весов внимания. На основе этого предложен метод VIVID, который использует усиленные визуальные значения, чтобы отбирать и уточнять поправки учителя для on-policy дистилляции от CE к NC. Abstract An attention-based process reward model that rewards internal reasoning through hidden states and attention maps improves reinforcement learning for large language models, yielding an average pass@8 gain of 2.88%.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Reinforcement learning (RL) has become an important component in large language models (LLMs). However, existing RL approaches focus on final outputs or textual solution trajectories, leading to coarse-grained rewards and unreliable reasoning. Such paradigms focus mainly on what the model outputs while overlooking how the model internally reasons. Therefore, can we reward how a model thinks internally rather than merely what it says? We first reveal the problem of textual reward aliasing in existing reward modeling approaches. Motivated by this, we propose the Attention Process Reward Model (APRM), which assigns internal rewards based on hidden states and attention maps to capture high-quality reasoning patterns. With only approximately 46M parameters, APRM is trained using ranking and counterfactual objectives, and its reward guides RL optimization while being periodically updated to handle distribution shifts. Integrated with representative RL methods, APRM achieved an average pass@8 gain of 2.88\%. Our results demonstrate the promise of incorporating internal reasoning signals into RL for LLMs.

Abstract A task-aware multimodal memory framework consolidates experience from completed tasks using source-linked multi-view memory, source-generated tasks, distillation, and task-aware retrieval, yielding consistent gains across memory benchmarks.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash AI agents accumulate conversations, images, and documents through long-term interactions with users. Answering later questions often requires connecting evidence across these records, yet the resulting understanding is rarely retained, allowing mistakes to recur on related questions. Existing multimodal memory methods largely focus on storing and retrieving records, with limited mechanisms for learning from completed tasks and consolidating task-specific experience. We therefore propose MMMem, a task-aware multimodal memory framework that extracts and consolidates experience from completed tasks through four complementary strategies. First, source-linked multi-view memory organizes original text, images, and rendered pages alongside generated scene images that combine clues across records for retrieval. Second, source-generated tasks support knowledge alignment at two levels: warm-up consolidates source knowledge into task-relevant experiences in external memory, while self-training adapts the model's parametric knowledge to task demands. Third, the same vision-language model distills completed tasks into experiences that record the task context, conclusion, and support links. Finally, task-aware retrieval recalls source views and relevant experiences for answering and verification, while source-supported reuse guides long-term retention. Across three memory benchmarks and two vision-language readers, MMMem outperforms eight baselines. "Abstract A task-aware multimodal memory framework consolidates experience from completed tasks using source-linked multi-view memory, source-generated tasks, distillation, and task-aware retrieval, yielding consistent gains across memory benchmarks.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash AI agents accumulate conversations, images, and documents through long-term interactions with users. Answering later questions often requires connecting evidence across these records, yet the resulting understanding is rarely retained, allowing mistakes to recur on related questions. Existing multimodal memory methods largely focus on storing and retrieving records, with limited mechanisms for learning from completed tasks and consolidating task-specific experience. We therefore propose MMMem, a task-aware multimodal memory framework that extracts and consolidates experience from completed tasks through four complementary strategies. First, source-linked multi-view memory organizes original text, images, and rendered pages alongside generated scene images that combine clues across records for retrieval. Second, source-generated tasks support knowledge alignment at two levels: warm-up consolidates source knowledge into task-relevant experiences in external memory, while self-training adapts the model's parametric knowledge to task demands. Third, the same vision-language model distills completed tasks into experiences that record the task context, conclusion, and support links. Finally, task-aware retrieval recalls source views and relevant experiences for answering and verification, while source-supported reuse guides long-term retention. Across three memory benchmarks and two vision-language readers, MMMem outperforms eight baselines. With Qwen3.5-9B, MMMem achieves relative gains of 36.9%, 17.6%, and 15.2% over the best-performing baselines on LoCoMo, Mem-Gallery, and LooGLE v2, respectively. Ablations show consistent gains from training and memory warm-up, while case studies illustrate how individual components work in practice."

Abstract  Touchard and the author asked whether every valued hyperfield, in Krasner's sense with the norm axiom, is of the form H_ρ(K): a valued field K read up to relative error ρ. The question turns on the norm axiom. If a valued hyperfield is a quotient K^times/Tcup{0} of a field, the norm axiom forces T=1+m_ρ for a valuation on K. This completes the proof of a published proposition, and it makes the question equivalent to asking whether every Krasner valued hyperfield is such a quotient. For value group Z we prove directly that it is. Without the norm axiom the answer is negative. Layering a hyperfield over a non-trivial ordered abelian group gives a factor hyperfield exactly when the hyperfield itself is one, so Massouros's hyperfields yield hyperfields with a non-trivial valuation, satisfying all the other axioms, that are not factor hyperfields. The general question remains open.

Авторы показывают, что аксиома нормы вынуждает T = 1 + m_ρ, что делает задачу эквивалентной вопросу, являются ли все Krasner valued hyperfields факторами поля K^×/T ∪ {0}. Для группы значений Z ответ положительный, а без аксиомы нормы ответ отрицательный, поскольку существуют hyperfields, не являющиеся factor hyperfields. "Abstract  Touchard and the author asked whether every valued hyperfield, in Krasner's sense with the norm axiom, is of the form H_ρ(K): a valued field K read up to relative error ρ. The question turns on the norm axiom. If a valued hyperfield is a quotient K^times/Tcup{0} of a field, the norm axiom forces T=1+m_ρ for a valuation on K. This completes the proof of a published proposition, and it makes the question equivalent to asking whether every Krasner valued hyperfield is such a quotient. For value group Z we prove directly that it is. Without the norm axiom the answer is negative. Layering a hyperfield over a non-trivial ordered abelian group gives a factor hyperfield exactly when the hyperfield itself is one, so Massouros's hyperfields yield hyperfields with a non-trivial valuation, satisfying all the other axioms, that are not factor hyperfields. The general question remains open."

[24.11.2026 13:40] OpenRouter request. Model: anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5. Prompt: Read an abstract of the ML paper and return a JSON with fields: 'desc': explanation of the paper in Russian (4 sentences), use correct machine learning terms. Abstract  Optimized floating-point inference is not reproducible across environments: the same model on different hardware, compilers or thread counts can produce different bits. We present tritify, an open-source runtime that executes the forward pass of real models -- decoder-only language models, Mixture-of-Experts decoders, and the Segment Anything 2.1 image/video stack -- with the entire run-time path in exact integer arithmetic; floating point is confined to offline quantization and the float reference used for verification. We make three separate claims and verify each. (1) Cross-hardware reproducibility: the integer path produces bit-identical output run to run, across CPU instruction-set architectures, and across CPU/GPU, carried by a reproducing SHA-256 of the output. (2) Decision fidelity: the integer path reproduces the float reference's decision on the models and inputs tested, with the numeric exceptions measured; per-layer fidelity through sixteen layers of OLMoE-1B-7B and of a 106-billion-parameter MoE shows no fidelity cliff. (3) Location independence: a model's computation can be split across instruction sets, CPU and GPU, and networked machines with zero effect on the output bits. GLM-4.5-Air (106,852,251,264 parameters, 427 GB of integer codes) generated its answer across three machines, none of which can hold its integer codes in RAM, bit-identically to a single-host reference. The consequence is verifiable distributed inference: an untrusted server or a fleet of devices runs the model while an open scalar oracle re-derives the exact bytes; a guarantee against a dishonest server is future work. We do not claim a per-device speed-up, bit-equality of floating-valued intermediates, or confidentiality. Code: https://github.com/pelloran-inc/tritify
[24.11.2026 13:40] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет tritify — открытый runtime, который выполняет прямой проход (forward pass) реальных моделей, включая decoder-only LLM, MoE-декодеры и стек Segment Anything 2.1, целиком в целочисленной арифметике. Вещественные числа используются только при offline-квантизации и в float-референсе для проверки. Авторы показывают побитовую воспроизводимость вывода на разных CPU, CPU/GPU и в распределённых сетях, а также сохранение решений относительно float-эталона без заметного падения точности по слоям. Это позволяет проверяемый распределённый inference, где недоверенный сервер можно верифицировать открытым оракулом, хотя защита от нечестного сервера остаётся будущей работой.", "emoji": "🔢", "title": "Побитово одинаковый вывод ML-моделей на любом железе благодаря целочисленной арифметике"}

The consequence is verifiable distributed inference: an untrusted server or a fleet of devices runs the model while an open scalar oracle re-derives the exact bytes; a guarantee against a dishonest server is future work. We do not claim a per-device speed-up, bit-equality of floating-valued intermediates, or confidentiality. Code: https://github.com/pelloran-inc/tritify"

"Abstract  Optimized floating-point inference is not reproducible across environments: the same model on different hardware, compilers or thread counts can produce different bits. We present tritify, an open-source runtime that executes the forward pass of real models -- decoder-only language models, Mixture-of-Experts decoders, and the Segment Anything 2.1 image/video stack -- with the entire run-time path in exact integer arithmetic; floating point is confined to offline quantization and the float reference used for verification. We make three separate claims and verify each. (1) Cross-hardware reproducibility: the integer path produces bit-identical output run to run, across CPU instruction-set architectures, and across CPU/GPU, carried by a reproducing SHA-256 of the output. (2) Decision fidelity: the integer path reproduces the float reference's decision on the models and inputs tested, with the numeric exceptions measured; per-layer fidelity through sixteen layers of OLMoE-1B-7B and of a 106-billion-parameter MoE shows no fidelity cliff. (3) Location independence: a model's computation can be split across instruction sets, CPU and GPU, and networked machines with zero effect on the output bits. GLM-4.5-Air (106,852,251,264 parameters, 427 GB of integer codes) generated its answer across three machines, none of which can hold its integer codes in RAM, bit-identically to a single-host reference. The consequence is verifiable distributed inference: an untrusted server or a fleet of devices runs the model while an open scalar oracle re-derives the exact bytes; a guarantee against a dishonest server is future work. We do not claim a per-device speed-up, bit-equality of floating-valued intermediates, or confidentiality. Code: https://github.com/pelloran-inc/tritify"

Abstract UniVEP combines endpoint and velocity prediction with consistency supervision and switches from endpoint recovery to velocity refinement as noise decreases, improving success and efficiency in few-step robot action generation.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Flow-matching robot policies usually use one prediction target throughout action generation, even though recovering actions from noise and polishing nearly clean actions bring different challenges. We systematically compare velocity prediction (V), endpoint prediction (X), and velocity-weighted endpoint prediction (X--V) across multiple robot policies, benchmarks, and inference budgets. On StarVLA and LiLa-WAM, X--V works best for few-step generation, while V gains more from iterative refinement. We further uncover their complementary strengths across noise levels: clean actions have concentrated covariance spectra, endpoint predictions stay more stable under high noise, and velocity predictions offer more accurate corrections near clean actions. Inspired by these findings, we introduce UniVEP, which learns both prediction targets together with consistency supervision and switches from endpoint recovery to velocity refinement as noise drops. Each step only needs one network run, and joint training also boosts single-step endpoint generation. Tests on simulated and real-world manipulation show better success rates and efficiency. On RoboTwin, UniVEP hits 61.36% success with one StarVLA run, beating the best single-target baseline at sixteen runs (59.96%). With LiLa-WAM, two runs outperform the best sixteen-step single-target baseline and cut inference latency by 77.1%.

Авторы сравнивают предсказание скорости (V), предсказание конечной точки (X) и взвешенное по скорости предсказание (X--V), показывая, что X--V лучше для генерации за несколько шагов, а V эффективнее при итеративном уточнении. [24.11.2026 13:40] OpenRouter request. Model: anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5. Prompt: Read an abstract of the ML paper and return a JSON with fields: 'desc': explanation of the paper in Russian (4 sentences), use correct machine learning terms. Abstract  Asking a vision-language model (VLM) to think step by step often makes it worse at spatial questions, even though the image stays in context. We show that short chain-of-thought (CoT) turns a perception problem into a transcription problem. Once a VLM writes a spatial relation, its answer follows that sentence instead of the image, so whether CoT helps or hurts depends largely on whether the written relation is more accurate than answering directly. Studying 15 open VLMs (2B-38B) and six frontier closed models, we rule out token count, turn format, and visual forgetting in open models and trace the failure through three stages. First, writing re-weights visual cues, weakening a "lower is closer" shortcut in all 13 open models tested but also discarding valid cues, which produces false relations. Second, the written text takes over. With the model's own conclusion-removed rationale held fixed, swapping in an opposite-depth image moves the answer by 2-9 points on rendered twins (vs. up to 77 for direct answers) and by 3.0-3.4 points on average on natural photographs (vs. 20.5-69.6). Third, the concluding sentence can invert even a true relation, mainly on converse questions. Together, false relations and conclusion mis-mapping account for four in five CoT losses. Scale does not remove this bottleneck: Qwen3-VL-32B shows the same pattern. Frontier closed models are not hurt: they write relations as accurately as they answer, and three of six re-check the image after writing. The three queried with reasoning off lock in like open models, so re-grounding co-occurs with answer-turn reasoning; we identify answer-step re-grounding as the step open VLMs lack. In a single-model pilot, fine-tuning on fixed rationales with image-determined labels restores it on natural photographs. We open-sourced all our datasets and code at https://ming-zch.github.io/seeing-right-writing-wrong.
[24.11.2026 13:40] Response: {"desc": "Авторы показывают, что просьба к vision-language model (VLM) рассуждать пошагово ухудшает ответы на пространственные вопросы. Причина в том, что записанное промежуточное утверждение о взаимном расположении объектов становится источником ответа, и модель опирается на текст, а не на изображение. Ошибки возникают на трёх этапах: письмо перевзвешивает визуальные подсказки и порождает ложные отношения, текст перехватывает ответ, а заключительная фраза может инвертировать даже верное отношение. В отличие от открытых моделей, frontier closed models перепроверяют изображение после записи, и дообучение на фиксированных рассуждениях с метками, определяемыми изображением, частично восстанавливает эту способность.", "emoji": "🧭", "title": "Модель видит правильно, но пишет неверно: текст подменяет изображение"}

"Abstract  Asking a vision-language model (VLM) to think step by step often makes it worse at spatial questions, even though the image stays in context. We show that short chain-of-thought (CoT) turns a perception problem into a transcription problem. Once a VLM writes a spatial relation, its answer follows that sentence instead of the image, so whether CoT helps or hurts depends largely on whether the written relation is more accurate than answering directly. Studying 15 open VLMs (2B-38B) and six frontier closed models, we rule out token count, turn format, and visual forgetting in open models and trace the failure through three stages. First, writing re-weights visual cues, weakening a "lower is closer" shortcut in all 13 open models tested but also discarding valid cues, which produces false relations. Second, the written text takes over. With the model's own conclusion-removed rationale held fixed, swapping in an opposite-depth image moves the answer by 2-9 points on rendered twins (vs. up to 77 for direct answers) and by 3.0-3.4 points on average on natural photographs (vs. 20.5-69.6). Third, the concluding sentence can invert even a true relation, mainly on converse questions. Together, false relations and conclusion mis-mapping account for four in five CoT losses. Scale does not remove this bottleneck: Qwen3-VL-32B shows the same pattern. Frontier closed models are not hurt: they write relations as accurately as they answer, and three of six re-check the image after writing. The three queried with reasoning off lock in like open models, so re-grounding co-occurs with answer-turn reasoning; we identify answer-step re-grounding as the step open VLMs lack. In a single-model pilot, fine-tuning on fixed rationales with image-determined labels restores it on natural photographs. We open-sourced all our datasets and code at https://ming-zch.github.io/seeing-right-writing-wrong."

Abstract A simulation benchmark shows that robot manipulation policies often fail at non-average conveyor speeds, while past frames or speed inputs improve models when speed is linearly decodable.  Generated by deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash Robot policies must adapt to the speed of moving objects, yet existing benchmarks rarely measure this ability across diverse tasks and speeds. We introduce SWEEP-Bench, a simulation benchmark with 80 conveyor-belt manipulation tasks spanning 16 skill categories, each evaluated at 13 belt speeds (0.10-0.40 m/s). Objects are transported through physical contact with a visibly moving belt. Each task has a static counterpart, and each evaluation episode uses the same randomized scene at all 13 speeds. We fine-tune eight vision-language-action and world-action models on demonstrations spanning the full speed range, comparing observations from the current frame alone with those including two past frames. For six models, the success rate peaks at intermediate speeds, and their failures indicate that they tend to produce actions suited to the average demonstration speed rather than to the actual one. Past frames raise the success rate by 10-40 percentage points for three models, and linear probes show that the belt speed is also most linearly decodable from these three. Adding ground-truth speed input to four of the models improves all of them, and a speed estimated from the images keeps most of this gain. These results show that SWEEP-Bench exposes speed-dependent failures and suggest that past frames help only policies from which the speed is most decodable, such as those pretrained on multiple frames. Project page: https://damin3927.github.io/SWEEP-Bench
[24.11.2026 13:40] Response: {"desc": "Авторы представляют SWEEP-Bench — симуляционный бенчмарк из 80 задач манипуляции объектами на движущемся конвейере при 13 скоростях ленты (0.10–0.40 м/с). Восемь моделей типа vision-language-action и world-action дообучались на демонстрациях во всём диапазоне скоростей, и большинство из них показывает пик успешности на средних скоростях, то есть действия подстраиваются под среднюю скорость демонстраций, а не под реальную. Использование двух предыдущих кадров повышает успешность на 10–40 процентных пунктов у моделей, где скорость лучше всего декодируется линейным probing-методом. Добавление информации о скорости (истинной или оценённой по изображениям) улучшает все модели, что показывает важность явного учёта скорости для робототехнических политик.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Роботам нужно чувствовать скорость конвейера, а не полагаться на среднюю"}
